and let
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow F^{\times}
be a locally algebraic weight so that
\kappa_{alg}=-w_{0,M}w\nu_{alg}-(w_{0,M}w\rho+\rho)
is
M
-dominant.

By corollary
6.3.8
, we have morphisms:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{-,fs}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},-w_{0,M}\kappa_{alg}-2\rho_{nc},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}

and similarly for cuspidal cohomology. We remark that these morphisms are not Hecke equivariant. Namely, on
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}
we have an action of
T^{+}
with the property that
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
acts via
\nu
and on
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{-,fs}
, we have an action of
T^{-}
such that
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
acts by
-\nu
(see lemma
6.3.11
). We deduce (compare with section
6.2.11
) that there is a
T^{+}
-equivariant map:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}(-\nu_{alg})
and a
T^{-}
-equivariant map:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{-,fs}(\nu_{alg})\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},-w_{0,M}\kappa_{alg}-2\rho_{nc},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}

We can study these maps with the help of the locally analytic BGG resolution.

Theorem 6.5.5
.

In the setting above, there is a
\mathcal{H}_{p,m,0}^{+}
-equivariant spectral sequence
\mathbf{E}^{p,q}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+}
converging to finite slope overconvergent cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{p+q}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}
,
such that

\mathbf{E}_{w,1}^{p,q}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+}=

\oplus_{v\in W_{M},\ell(v)=p}\mathrm{H}^{q}_{w,an}\big(K^{p},(((w_{0,M}w)^{-1}vw_{0,M}w)\cdot\nu_{alg})\chi\big)^{+,fs}\big(-(((w_{0,M}w)^{-1}vw_{0,M}w)\cdot\nu_{alg})\big).

There is a
\mathcal{H}_{p,m,0}^{-}
-equivariant spectral sequence
\mathbf{E}^{p,q}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{-}
converging to finite slope overconvergent cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{p+q}_{w}(K^{p},-w_{0,M}\kappa_{alg}-2\rho_{nc},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}
,
such that

\mathbf{E}_{w,1}^{p,q}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{-}=

\oplus_{v\in W_{M},\ell(v)=-p}\mathrm{H}^{q}_{w,an}\big(K^{p},(((w_{0,M}w)^{-1}vw_{0,M}w)\cdot\nu_{alg})\chi\big)^{-,fs}\big((((w_{0,M}w)^{-1}vw_{0,M}w)\cdot\nu_{alg})\big).

Proof.

This is the spectral sequence associated to the BGG sequence of theorem
6.2.16
, as well as its variant for distribution sheaves. We use the following identities:
\nu_{alg}+\rho=-w^{-1}w_{0,M}(\kappa_{alg}+\rho)
and
-w^{-1}w_{0,M}(v.\cdot\kappa_{alg}+\rho)-\rho=((w_{0,M}w)^{-1}vw_{0,M}w)\cdot\nu_{alg}
.
∎

6.6.
Cohomological vanishing

The following is the analogue of theorem
5.6.1
.

Theorem 6.6.1
.

For all
w\in{}^{M}W
and weights
\nu_{A}
, the cohomology
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{\pm,fs}
is concentrated in degree
[0,\ell_{\pm}(w)]
.

Proof.

This is exactly the same as the proof of theorem
5.6.1
, granting lemmas
6.6.2
and
6.6.4
below.
∎

The key lemma to prove the theorem is the following (this lemma and its proof are inspired by
[
AIP15
]
):

Lemma 6.6.2
.

Assume that
(G,X)
is a Hodge type Shimura datum. Suppose the weight
\nu_{A}
is
n
-analytic, and let
K_{p}\subseteq K_{p,m^{\prime},0}
for some
m^{\prime}>n
. For all affinoid opens
\mathcal{U}\subseteq]C_{w,k}[_{n,n}K_{p}
,
\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi_{HT,K_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U}),\mathcal{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}(-D))
and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi_{HT,K_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U}),\mathcal{D}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}(-D))
are concentrated in degree
0
.

Proof.

We only consider the case of the sheaf
\mathcal{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}(-D)
, the other case is identical. For any compact open
K_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq K_{p}
, let
\mathfrak{S}^{tor}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},\Sigma}
be an integral toroidal compactification, and let
\mathfrak{S}^{\star}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}
be an integral minimal compactification (whose existence is given by the main results of
[
MP19
]
, see also section
4.4.31
). Let
\mathfrak{U}
be a formal model for
\mathcal{U}
, realized as an open subset of the normalization of a blow-up of
\mathfrak{FL}_{G,\mu}
. By theorem
4.4.36
,
for
K_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq K_{p}
small enough, we have normal formal models
\mathfrak{S}^{\star,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},\mathfrak{U}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}^{\star}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}
for the map
\pi_{HT,K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime}}
and
\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}^{tor}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},\Sigma}
for the map
(\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p}^{\prime}})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{tor}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},\Sigma}
and we have a map
f_{K_{p}^{\prime}}:\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}^{\star,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},\mathfrak{U}}
.

We may actually assume that
K_{p}^{\prime}
is a subgroup of the pro
p
-group
K_{p,m^{\prime},1}
.
By proposition
4.6.15
for
K_{p}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq K_{p}^{\prime}
small enough, the torsor
\mathcal{M}_{HT,n,K_{p,m^{\prime},1}}

is trivial on the generic fiber of a Zariski cover of
\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}
.

We can construct a formal model for the Banach sheaf
\mathcal{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}
that we denote
\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}
over
\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}
. Indeed let
\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}=\cup_{i}\mathfrak{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},i}
be a Zariski covering with the property that the torsor
\mathcal{M}_{HT,n,K_{p,m^{\prime},1}}
is trivial over the generic fiber
\mathcal{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},i}
of
\mathfrak{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},i}
. We fix such a trivialization. We can construct the associated
1
-cocycle: over each intersection
\mathfrak{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},i,j}=\mathfrak{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},i}\cap\mathfrak{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},j}
we have an element
m_{i,j}\in K_{p,m^{\prime},1,w,M_{\mu}}^{c}\mathcal{M}_{\mu,n}^{c}
describing the change of trivialization of the torsor
\mathcal{M}_{HT,n,K_{p,m^{\prime},1}}
. Over
\mathfrak{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},i}
we let
\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}=V_{\kappa_{A}}^{n-an,+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},i}}
and we glue these sheaves using multiplication by
m_{i,j}
on each intersection. The sheaf
\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}
is a flat formal Banach sheaf (see section
2.5.5
). We claim that it is also small (see again section
2.5.5
). Indeed, it follows from lemma
6.2.6
that the representation
V_{\kappa_{A}}^{n-an,+}\otimes_{A^{+}}A^{+}/A^{++}
of
K_{p,w,M_{\mu}}^{c}\mathcal{M}_{\mu,n}^{c}
is an inductive limit of finite free
A^{+}/A^{++}
-submodules
\mathrm{colim}_{i}V_{i}
, stable under
K_{p,w,M_{\mu}}^{c}\mathcal{M}_{\mu,n}^{c}
, and such that over
V_{i}/V_{i-1}
the action factors over the quotient
K_{p,w,M_{\mu}}^{c,p}
of
K_{p,w,M_{\mu}}^{c}
by its maximal normal pro-
p
subgroup. We find that
K_{p,w,M_{\mu}}^{c,p}=wT^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})w^{-1}/wT_{1}^{c}w^{-1}
is a finite abelian group. Moreover, the elements
m_{i,j}
map trivially to
K_{p,w,M_{\mu}}^{c,p}
.

It follows that the sheaf
\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}\otimes_{A^{+}}A^{+}/A^{++}=\mathrm{colim}_{i}\mathscr{F}_{i}
is an inductive limit of locally free sheaves of
A^{+}/A^{++}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}}
-modules with the property that
\mathscr{F}_{i}/\mathscr{F}_{i-1}
is the trivial sheaf. We now fix
\mathcal{L}
an ample line bundle over
\mathfrak{S}^{\star,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\mathfrak{U}}
(this last formal scheme is quasi-projective, so
\mathcal{L}
exists).

We claim that there exists
m\geq 0
such that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}},f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{L}^{m}\otimes\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}(-D_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}))
(where
-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}}
is the boundary divisor at level
K^{\prime\prime}_{p}
) is concentrated in degree
0
.
The cohomology is represented by a complex of
A^{+}
-modules which are completions of free
A^{+}
-modules (take the Čech complex associated with the covering
\cup\mathfrak{V}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},i}
). It follows from lemma
6.6.3
below that it suffices to prove that there exists
m\geq 0
such that

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}},f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{L}^{m}\otimes\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}/A^{++}(-D_{K_{p}}))
is concentrated in degree
0
. We are therefore reduced to prove that there exists
m
such that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}},f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{L}^{m}\otimes\mathscr{F}_{i}/\mathscr{F}_{i-1}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}}))
is concentrated in degree
0
. By the projection formula,

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}},f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{L}^{m}\otimes\mathscr{F}_{i}/\mathscr{F}_{i-1}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}}))=\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{S}^{\star,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}},\mathcal{L}^{m}\otimes\mathrm{R}(f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}})_{\star}\mathscr{F}_{i}/\mathscr{F}_{i-1}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}})).

We first observe that
\mathrm{R}(f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}})_{\star}\mathscr{F}_{i}/\mathscr{F}_{i-1}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}})
is a sheaf concentrated in degree
0
by theorem
4.4.37
.
Since
\mathcal{L}
is very ample on
\mathfrak{S}^{\star,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}
and
(f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}})_{\star}\mathscr{F}_{i}/\mathscr{F}_{i-1}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}})
is a coherent sheaf independant of
i
, it follows that there exists
m
such that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}},f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{L}^{m}\otimes\mathscr{F}_{i}/\mathscr{F}_{i-1}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}}))
is concentrated in degree
0
for all
i
.

Let
L=\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathfrak{S}^{\star,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\mathfrak{U}},\mathcal{L}[1/p])
. This is a rank
1
projective
\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\star}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}}((\pi_{HT,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}})^{-1}(\mathcal{U}))
-module. We observe that

f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{L}^{m}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}}}\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}})[1/p]=L^{m}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\pi_{HT,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})}}\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}})[1/p].

We deduce that

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}},f_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}\mathcal{L}^{m}\otimes\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}})[1/p])=

L^{m}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\pi_{HT,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U})}}\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\pi_{HT,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}),\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}})[1/p])

and therefore
\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}},\mathfrak{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}})[1/p])
is concentrated in degree
0
. Taking a Zariski covering of
\mathfrak{S}^{tor,mod}_{K^{p}K_{p}^{\prime\prime},\Sigma,\mathfrak{U}}
, we deduce that the associated augmented Čech complex on the generic fiber is exact. By theorem
2.5.9
, affinoids are acyclic for Banach sheaves arisings as generic fibers of flat small formal Banach sheaves. We deduce from the Čech to cohomology spectral sequence that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi_{HT,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U}),\mathcal{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an,+}(-D_{K^{\prime\prime}_{p}}))
is concentrated in degree
0
. The lemma follows by taking the invariants under
K_{p}/K_{p}^{\prime\prime}
.

∎

Lemma 6.6.3
.

Let
A^{+}
be a ring,
A^{+}+
be an ideal of
A^{+}
. Assume that
A^{+}
is
A^{+}+
-adically complete and separated, Let
M_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{0}}}{{\rightarrow}}M_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{1}}}{{\rightarrow}}M_{2}
be a complex of complete, separated, and torsion free
A^{+}
-modules. Assume that

M_{0}\otimes_{A^{+}}A^{+}/A^{++}\rightarrow M_{1}\otimes_{A^{+}}A^{+}/A^{++}\rightarrow M_{2}\otimes_{A^{+}}A^{+}/A^{++}

is exact. Then the complex
M_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{0}}}{{\rightarrow}}M_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{1}}}{{\rightarrow}}M_{2}
is exact.

Proof.

It follows from the assumptions that
\mathrm{Im}(f_{0})+(A^{++}M_{1}\cap\mathrm{Ker}(f_{1}))=\mathrm{Ker}(f_{1})
. Since
M_{2}
is torsion free, we deduce that

(A^{++}M_{1}\cap\mathrm{Ker}(f_{1}))=A^{++}\mathrm{Ker}(f_{1})
. Let
m\in\mathrm{Ker}(f_{1})
. By successive approximation, we can construct a sequence of elements
m_{n}\in M_{0}
for
n\geq 0
, with
m_{n}-m_{n+1}\in(A^{++})^{n}M_{0}
and
f_{0}(m_{n})-m\in\mathrm{(}A^{++})^{n+1}M_{1}
. The sequence
m_{n}
converges to
m_{\infty}
and
f_{0}(m_{\infty})=m
.
∎

Lemma 6.6.4
.

Lemma
6.6.2
holds without assuming that
(G,X)
is an Hodge type Shimura datum.

Proof.

We consider a diagram of Shimura datum with
(G,X)
of abelian type and
(G_{1},X_{1})
of Hodge type as in section
4.4.41
:

\textstyle{(B_{1},X_{B_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(B,X_{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(G_{1},X_{1})}
\textstyle{(G,X)}

We may assume that the various morphisms between the groups
G_{1},B_{1},B,G
over
\mathbb{Q}
extend to morphisms over
\mathcal{O}_{F}
.
We will prove that lemma
6.6.2
holds first for
B_{1}
and second for
G
. We add decorations
H
or
(H,X_{H})
to objects depending on the Shimura datum
(H,X_{H})
in order to make the distinction between the Shimura data.
Let
\nu_{A}:T(B_{1})(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow A^{\times}
be a continuous,
n
-analytic character. It induces a character
\nu^{\prime}_{A}:T(G_{1})(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow A^{\times}
.
We consider the morphism of compact open subgroups
K_{p}=K_{p,m^{\prime},0}(B_{1})\rightarrow K^{\prime}_{p}=K_{p,m^{\prime},0}(G_{1})
and
we deduce a finite morphism of Shimura varieties:

f:\mathcal{S}^{tor}(B_{1},X_{B_{1}})_{K,\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{tor}(G_{1},X_{G_{1}})_{K^{\prime},\Sigma}

with
K=K^{p}K_{p}
and
K^{\prime}=(K^{\prime})^{p}K^{\prime}_{p}
and by restriction (see theorem
4.4.45
, lemma
3.5.6
) we have a finite morphism:

f:(\pi_{HT,K_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{n,n}K_{p})\rightarrow(\pi_{HT,K^{\prime}_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{n,n}K^{\prime}_{p})

We claim that there is an isomorphism

f^{\star}\mathcal{V}_{\nu^{\prime}_{A}}^{n-an}=\mathcal{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}.

This follows from remark
4.6.6
and remark
4.6.13
, observing that the torsor comming from the Shimura variety
(T,X_{T})
is trivial. We insist that the above formula does not describe the Hecke action at the level of
B_{1}
, but this is irrelevant for us now.
We can the find another compact
K^{\prime\prime}\subseteq K^{\prime}
such that there is a finite, generically finite étale morphism
f:\mathcal{S}^{tor,0}(G_{1},X_{G_{1}})_{K^{\prime\prime},\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{tor,0}(B_{1},X_{B_{1}})_{K,\Sigma}
with group
\Delta
.
We now deduce that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi_{HT,K_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})^{0},\mathcal{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}(-D))
is the direct factor of invariants under
\Delta
of
\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi_{HT,K^{\prime\prime}_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})^{0},\mathcal{V}_{\nu^{\prime}_{A}}^{n-an}(-D))
. We wrote
(\pi_{HT,K_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})^{0}
for
\mathcal{S}^{tor,0}(B_{1},X_{B_{1}})_{K,\Sigma}\cap(\pi_{HT,K_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})
and similarly for
G_{1}
. The lemma is thus proven for
B_{1}
. We observe that the lemma holds for any refinement of the cone decomposition
\Sigma
by the projection formula.
We now deduce the lemma for
G
. We reset the notations. Let
\nu_{A}:T(G)(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow A^{\times}
be a continuous,
n
-analytic character. It induces a character
\nu^{\prime}_{A}:T(B_{1})(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow A^{\times}
.
Let
K=K^{p}K_{p}
be a compact open subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
We can find a compact open subgroup
K^{\prime}\subseteq B_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that there is a finite, generically finite étale morphism
f:\mathcal{S}^{tor,0}(B_{1},X_{B_{1}})_{K^{\prime},\Sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}^{tor,0}(G,X_{G})_{K,\Sigma}
with group
\Delta(K,K^{\prime})
.
This map induces a finite map :
f:(\pi_{HT,K^{\prime}_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{n,n}K^{\prime}_{p})^{0}\rightarrow(\pi_{HT,K_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{n,n}K_{p})^{0}
. We find that

\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}=(f_{\star}\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu^{\prime}_{A}})^{\Delta(K,K^{\prime})}.

We deduce that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi_{HT,K_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})^{0},\mathcal{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}(-D))
is the direct factor of invariants under
\Delta(K,K^{\prime})
of
\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi_{HT,K^{\prime}_{p}}^{tor})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})^{0},\mathcal{V}_{\nu^{\prime}_{A}}^{n-an}(-D))
. The lemma is thus proven for
G
.
∎

6.7.
Cup products and Serre duality

We now consider cup-products on locally analytic overconvergent cohomology.

Theorem 6.7.1
.

For all
w\in{}^{M}W
and weights
\nu_{A}
there is a pairing:

\langle,\rangle:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K_{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{\pm,fs}\times\mathrm{H}^{d-i}_{w,an}(K_{p},\nu_{A})^{\mp,fs}\rightarrow A

For any
t\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
we have
\langle t-,-\rangle=\langle-,t^{-1}-\rangle
.
Let
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
be a locally algebraic weight so that
\kappa_{alg}=-w_{0,M}w\nu_{alg}-(w_{0,M}w\rho+\rho)
is
M
-dominant. The above pairing induces a pairing between the spectral sequences:

\langle,\rangle_{p,q,r}:\mathbf{E}_{w,r}^{p,q}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi,cusp)^{\pm}\times\mathbf{E}^{-p,d-q}_{w,r}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi^{-1})^{\mp}\rightarrow F

On the abutment of the spectral sequence the pairing
\langle,\rangle_{p,q,\infty}
is induced by the pairing of theorem
5.7.1
:

\mathrm{H}^{p+q}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi,cusp)^{\pm,fs}\times\mathrm{H}^{d-p-q}_{w}(K^{p},-2\rho_{nc}-w_{0,M}\kappa,\chi^{-1})^{\mp,fs}\rightarrow F.

Proof.

We construct the pairing

\langle,\rangle:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K_{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{+,fs}\times\mathrm{H}^{d-i}_{w,an}(K_{p},\nu_{A})^{-,fs}\rightarrow F.

We can realize
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{+,fs}
as the the finite slope part of

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))

for
s>>0
and
m^{\prime}>>s
by definition
6.4.7
and theorem
6.4.8
. Similarly we can realize
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A})^{-,fs}
as the finite slope part of

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s+1},s-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{-1,s-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{D}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}).

Moreover, by construction we have a pairing
\mathcal{V}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}(-D)\times\mathcal{D}_{\nu_{A}}^{n-an}\to\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}}(-D)\otimes A
. We have a cup-product by proposition
2.2.1
:

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))\times

\mathrm{H}^{d-i}_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s+1},s-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{-1,s-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{D}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})

\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{d}_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s+1},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,s-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}}(-D)\otimes A)

and there is a trace map (by theorem
2.7.1
):

\mathrm{H}^{d}_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s+1},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,s-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}}(-D)\otimes A)\rightarrow A.

This pairing intertwines the actions of
\mathcal{H}_{p,m,0}^{+}
and
\mathcal{H}_{p,m,0}^{-}
. It is straightforward (and painful) to check that the induced pairing

\langle,\rangle:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K_{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{+,fs}\times\mathrm{H}^{d-i}_{w,an}(K_{p},\nu_{A})^{-,fs}\rightarrow A

is independent of choices.

The rest of the theorem follows from the functoriality of the trace map.

∎

Theorem 6.7.2
.

Let
w\in{}^{M}W
, let
A
be a field and fix a weight
\nu_{A}
.
The pairing
\langle,\rangle:\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K_{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{\pm,fs}\times\mathrm{H}^{d-i}_{w,an}(K_{p},\nu_{A})^{\mp,fs}\rightarrow A
is non-degenerate.

Proof.

We follow closely the proof of theorem
5.7.2
. Our strategy is to express the cohomologies as cones of cohomologies (possibly with compact support) of dagger spaces and to use the duality for affinoid dagger spaces to produce dual complexes whose finite slope part computes the cohomology.
We can realize
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{+,fs}
as the the finite slope part of

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))

for
s>>0
and
m^{\prime}>>s
by definition
6.4.7
and theorem
6.4.8

We now introduce the following closed subspaces of
\mathcal{FL}_{G,\mu}
:

]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s-1}^{\prime},\overline{s}}=\cap_{\epsilon>0}]C_{w,k}[_{s-1-\epsilon,\overline{s}},~\textrm{and}~]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},\overline{s}}=]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s-1}^{\prime},\overline{s}}\setminus]C_{w,k}[_{s,\overline{s}}.

Both are the closure of quasi-compact open subspaces of
\mathcal{FL}_{G,\mu}
. We have an exact triangle:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s-2,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))\rightarrow

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s-1}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s-2,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))

\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\rightarrow}}

We observe that
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s-2,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))
and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s-1}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s-2,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))
are the cohomology with compact support of the dagger spaces
(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})
and
(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s-1}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})
respectively.

For the
-
theory, we see that for any
\epsilon>0
, the cohomology

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s},s-\epsilon}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{-1,s-\epsilon}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}})

is quasi-isomorphic to the cone of

\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s}^{\prime},s-\epsilon}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}})[-1]\rightarrow

\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},s-\epsilon}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}})[-1]

We point out that in this argument, in order to apply duality theorems, we prefer to use as coefficients
(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}}
rather than
\mathcal{D}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}
. There is a map
\mathcal{D}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}}
. When we pass to the inverse limit on
n
, these maps induce an isomorphism. Since
A
is a field,
(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}}
is a projective Banach sheaf.

Passing to the limit over
\epsilon
, we deduce easily that the finite slope part of the cone of

\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}})[-1]\rightarrow

\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}})[-1]

computes
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A})^{-,fs}
.

We can consider a finite covering
\cup_{i}U_{i}^{\dagger}
of
(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s-1}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})
by affinoid dagger spaces (or equivalently by closed spaces which are the closure of quasi-compact open spaces) and we let
\cup_{j}V_{j}^{\dagger}
be a covering of
(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})
by affinoid dagger spaces, refining
\cup_{i}(U_{i}^{\dagger}\cap(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}))
.

We let
A^{\bullet}=\check{C}_{c}(\{U_{i}^{\dagger}\},\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))
and
B^{\bullet}=\check{C}_{c}(\{V_{i}^{\dagger}\},\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D))

be the Čhech cohomology complex with compact support. There is a natural map
B^{\bullet}\rightarrow A^{\bullet}
and we let
C^{\bullet}
be the cone of this map which represents

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{(\pi_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}}^{tor})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,\overline{s}}K_{p,m^{\prime},0})}((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{s,-1}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}}(-D)).

Let
t\in T^{++}
. As explained in lemma
5.2.6
, we can lift
t
to a compact endomorphism of
C^{\bullet}
.

We let
D^{\bullet}
be the complex which is the strict dual of
C^{\bullet}
. By the duality for affinoid spaces, this complex computes the cone of

\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{s}^{\prime},s-\epsilon}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}})[-1]\rightarrow

\mathrm{R}\Gamma((\pi^{tor}_{HT,K_{p,m^{\prime},0}})^{-1}(]C_{w,k}[_{\overline{[s-1,s]}^{\prime},s-\epsilon}K_{p,m^{\prime},0}),(\mathcal{V}^{n-an}_{\nu_{A}})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{-2\rho_{nc}})[-1].

For any slope
h
, taking the slope
\leq h
part yields a perfect pairing between
C^{\bullet,\leq h}
and
D^{\bullet,\leq h}
and passing to cohomology gives the theorem.

∎

Using duality we can deduce an improved vanishing theorem.

Theorem 6.7.3
.

Let
w\in{}^{M}W
and let
\nu_{A}
be a weight. The cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{\pm,fs}
is concentrated in degree
[0,\ell_{\pm}(w)]
and the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A})^{\pm,fs}
is concentrated in degree
[\ell_{\pm}(w),d]
. Moreover, if
A
is a domain,
\mathrm{H}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A})^{\pm,fs}
is a torsion free sheaf of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathrm{Spa}(A,A^{+})}
-modules.

Proof.

The case of
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A},cusp)^{\pm,fs}
is theorem
6.6.1
. The case of
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A})^{\pm,fs}
when
A
is a field follows from the duality theorem
6.7.2
. The case of general coefficients can be deduced as follows. First observe that we can suppose that
A
is reduced as the universal weight space is reduced. Let us fix a compact operator
t\in T^{\pm}
. For any point
x\in\mathrm{Spa}(A,A^{+})
and any slope
h
, we can find a slope
h^{\prime}\geq h
and an open
\mathrm{Spa}(B,B^{+})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spa}(A,A^{+})
such that
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A})|_{\mathrm{Spa}(B,B)^{+}}
has slope
h^{\prime}
decomposition for
t
, and
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A})(\mathrm{Spa}(B,B)^{+})^{\pm,\leq h^{\prime}}
is computed by a perfect complex
C^{\bullet}
of
B
-modules. Since we know that for any
x\in\mathrm{Spa}(B,B^{+})
,
C^{\bullet}\otimes_{B}k(x)
has cohomology concentrated in the range
[\ell_{\pm}(w),d]
, we deduce that
C^{\bullet}
is quasi-isomorphic to
\tau_{\geq\ell_{\pm}(w)}C^{\bullet}
, and this truncated complex is a perfect complex (by an application of
[
Mum08
]
, lemma 2 on p. 49). It follows that its cohomology in degree
\ell_{\pm}(w)
is torsion free if
A
is a domain.
∎

6.8.
Slope estimates and control theorem

We now formulate a conjecture regarding the slopes of the locally analytic overconvergent cohomologies. This is of course consistent with conjecture
5.9.2
.

Conjecture 6.8.1
.

Fix
w\in{}^{M}W
, and let
\nu:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
be a continuous character.

(1)

For any character
\lambda
of
T^{+}
on
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}
or
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu,cusp)^{+,fs}
, we have
v(\lambda)\geq 0
.

(2)

For any character
\lambda
of
T^{-}
on
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{-,fs}
or
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu,cusp)^{-,fs}
, we have
v(\lambda)\leq 0
.

Remark 6.8.2
.

The inequalities in conjecture
6.8.1
are compatible with those of conjecture
5.9.2
due to the way we have renormalized the Hecke operators acting on locally analytic cohomology. Similarly the slightly weaker bounds we prove in theorem
6.8.3
are compatible with those of theorem
5.9.6
.

We can prove a bound which is slightly weaker than the conjecture.

Theorem 6.8.3
.

Fix
w\in{}^{M}W
, and let
\nu:T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
be a continuous character.

(1)

For any character
\lambda
of
T^{+}
on
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}
and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu,cusp)^{+,fs}
, we have
v(\lambda)\geq-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\rho-\rho
and
v(\lambda)\geq-w^{-1}w_{0,M}\rho-\rho+w^{-1}2\rho_{nc}

(2)

For any character
\lambda
of
T^{-}
on
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{-,fs}
and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu,cusp)^{-,fs}
, we have
v(\lambda)\leq-w^{-1}\rho+\rho
and
v(\lambda)\leq-w^{-1}\rho+w^{-1}2\rho_{nc}
.

Proof.

This is similar to the proof of theorem
5.9.6
and left to the reader.
∎

Corollary 6.8.4
.

Fix
w\in{}^{M}W
, and let
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
be a locally algebraic character. Let
\kappa_{alg}=-w_{0,M}w\nu_{alg}-(w_{0,M}w\rho+\rho)
and suppose
\kappa_{alg}\in X^{\star}(T)^{M,+}
. Then the morphisms

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,sss_{M,w}(\kappa_{alg})}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)(-\nu_{alg})^{+,sss_{M,w}(\kappa_{alg})}

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)(\nu_{alg})^{-,sss_{M,w}(-w_{0,M}\kappa_{alg}-2\rho_{nc})}\rightarrow\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{w}(K^{p},-w_{0,M}\kappa-2\rho_{nc},\chi^{-1})^{-,sss_{M,w}(-w_{0,M}\kappa_{alg}-2\rho_{nc})}

and the corresponding morphisms for cuspidal cohomology are all quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

This follows from the spectral sequence of theorem
6.5.5
, together with the slope bounds of theorem
6.8.3
.
∎

Remark 6.8.5
.

If we assume conjectures
5.9.2
and
6.8.1
, we can replace the
sss_{M,w}
condition by the
ss_{M,w}
condition in the above corollary.

6.9.
Eigenvarieties

Consider the Iwasawa algebra
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})]]
and the weight space

\mathcal{W}=\mathrm{Spa}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})]],\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})]])\times_{\mathrm{Spa}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\mathrm{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

For a
(A,A^{+})
a complete Tate
(F,\mathcal{O}_{F})
-algebra,

\mathrm{Hom}(\mathrm{Spa}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{W})=\{\textrm{Continuous characters}~\nu_{A}:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow A^{\times}\}.

Also let
\widehat{T}
be the analytic adic space of characters of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, whose restriction to
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
factor through
T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
If we fix a splitting
\xi
for the map
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and we fix an isomorphism
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{r}
(for
r=\mathrm{rank}(X^{\star}(T^{d}))
), then we have an isomorphism
\widehat{T}\simeq\mathcal{W}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{an})^{r}
, where the map sends a character
\lambda
to
(\lambda|_{T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})},\lambda(\xi(e_{1})),\cdots,\lambda(\xi(e_{r})))
(for the canonical basis
e_{1},\cdots,e_{r}
of
\mathbb{Z}^{r}
).
We also observe that there is a natural map
T\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\widehat{T}}
.

Let
\mathrm{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset\mathcal{W}
be an affinoid open and let
\nu_{A}^{un}:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow A^{\times}
be the universal character.

For each
w\in{}^{M}W
we have sheaves over
\mathrm{Spa}(A,A^{+})
:
\oplus_{i}\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A}^{un})^{\pm,fs}
and
\oplus_{i}\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu_{A}^{un},cusp)^{\pm,fs}
.
Gluing these sheaves for an affinoid covering of
\mathcal{W}
we deduce that there are sheaves of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-modules
\oplus_{i=\ell_{\pm}(w)}^{d}\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{\pm,fs}
and
\oplus_{i=0}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)^{\pm,fs}
, admitting slope decomposition.

Remark 6.9.1
.

Our weight variable
\nu
is morally a translate of the infinitesimal character. We recall how to switch between the weight of our coherent cohomologies and the infinitesimal character. Let
\nu=\nu^{alg}\chi
be a locally algebraic weight.
Then
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{+,fs}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}k(\nu)
and
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)^{+,fs}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}k(\nu)
are related to classical or overconvergent cohomology in weight
\kappa_{alg}
where
\nu_{alg}=-w^{-1}w_{0,M}(\kappa_{alg}+\rho)-\rho
.
On the other hand,
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{-,fs}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}k(\nu)
and
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)^{-,fs}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}k(\nu)
are related to classical or overconvergent cohomology in weight
\kappa_{alg}^{\vee}=-w_{0,M}\kappa_{alg}-2\rho_{nc}
and
\nu_{alg}=w^{-1}(\kappa_{alg}^{\vee}+\rho)-\rho
.

We also have cup-products:

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{\pm,fs}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{d-i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cup)^{\mp,fs}\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}.

We modify the action of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{-,fs}
and
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)^{-,fs}
, by composing it with the inverse map
t\mapsto t^{-1}
. With this modification, the operators
t\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
are self adjoint for the pairing.
The action of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on the various sheaves
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{\pm,fs}
and
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)^{\pm,fs}
over
\mathcal{W}
produces a spectral variety, which is a Zariski closed subset
\mathcal{Z}\hookrightarrow\widehat{T}
(see section
6.1
). We have coherent sheaves
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}
and
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}
of
\mathscr{O}_{\widehat{T}}
-modules supported on
\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{Z}}
acts faithfully on the direct sum of all these sheaves. Moreover, if
\pi:\widehat{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}
is the projection to the weight space,

\pi_{\star}\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}=\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{\pm,fs}
and
\pi_{\star}\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}=\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)^{\pm,fs}
.

We assume that
K^{p}=\prod K_{\ell}
and we let
S^{\prime}
be the set of primes
\ell\neq p
such that
K_{\ell}
is not hyperspecial. We let
S=S^{\prime}\cup\{p\}
. Let
\mathcal{H}^{S}=\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{S})//K^{S},\mathbb{Q})
be the spherical Hecke algebra at places away from
S
. We let
\mathcal{E}
be the finite adic space over
\mathcal{Z}
whose algebra is the coherent
\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{Z}}
-algebra generated by the operators
h\in\mathcal{H}^{S}
acting on the sum of the sheaves
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}
and
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}
, for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, all
w\in{}^{M}W
, and all choices of
+
or
-
.

A (classical rigid analytic) point of
\mathcal{Z}
corresponds to a character
\lambda_{p}:T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
. We can attach to
\lambda_{p}
the weight
\nu=\lambda_{p}|_{T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
. When the weight
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
is locally algebraic, then we let
\lambda_{p}^{sm}=\lambda_{p}\nu_{alg}^{-1}
with
\nu_{alg}
viewed as a character of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Then
\lambda_{p}^{sm}
factors through a character of
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/T_{b}
where
b
is the conductor of
\chi
.

Remark 6.9.2
.

The superscript
{sm}
stands for smooth, because the character
\lambda_{p}^{sm}
is the smooth character attached to
\lambda_{p}
. Remark that the Hecke action on classical cohomology produces smooth characters. This is visible in point
(1)
of theorem
6.9.3
below.

A point of
\mathcal{E}
corresponds to a pair
(\lambda_{p},\lambda^{S})
where
\lambda^{S}:\mathcal{H}^{S}\to\mathbb{C}_{p}
is a character.

Theorem 6.9.3
.

The eigenvariety
\pi:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}
is locally quasi-finite and partially proper. It carries graded coherent sheaves

\bigoplus_{w\in{}^{M}W,k\in\mathbb{Z}}(\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{+,fs}\oplus\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{-,fs}

\oplus\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{+,fs}\oplus\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{-,fs})

and they satisfy the following properties:

(1)

(Any classical, finite slope eigenclass gives a point of the eigenvariety) For any
\kappa_{alg}\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{M,+}
, finite order character
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\overline{F}^{\times}
, and any system of Hecke eigenvalues
(\lambda^{sm}_{p},\lambda^{S})
occurring in
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}
(resp.
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg}^{\vee},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}
,
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi,cusp)^{+,fs}
, or
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg}^{\vee},\chi^{-1},cusp)^{-,fs}
) there is a
w=w_{M}w^{M}\in W
, so that if
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
with
\nu_{alg}=-w^{-1}w_{0,M}(\kappa_{alg}+\rho)-\rho
, then
(\lambda^{sm}_{p}\nu_{alg},\lambda^{S})
is a point of the eigenvariety
\mathcal{E}
which lies in the support of
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w^{M},an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{+,fs}
(resp.
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w^{M},an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{-,fs}
,
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w^{M},an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{+,fs}
, or
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w^{M},an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{-,fs}
).

(2)

(Small slope points of the eigenvariety in regular, locally algebraic weights are classical) Conversely if
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
is a locally algebraic weight with
\nu_{alg}\in X^{\star}(T)^{+}
, and
(\lambda_{p},\lambda^{S})
is a point of
\mathcal{E}
in the support of
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{+,fs}
(resp.
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{-,fs}
,
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{+,fs}
, or
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{-,fs}
) for some
w\in{}^{M}W
, and if
\lambda_{p}^{sm}=\lambda_{p}\nu_{alg}^{-1}
satisfies
+,sss_{w}(\nu)
then
(\lambda^{sm}_{p},\lambda^{S})
occurs in
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}
(resp.
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa^{\vee}_{alg},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}
,
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi,cusp)^{+,fs}
, or
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa^{\vee}_{alg},\chi^{-1},cusp)^{-,fs}
) for
\kappa_{alg}=-w_{0,M}w(\nu_{alg}+\rho)-\rho
.

(3)

(Serre duality interpolates over the eigenvariety) We have pairings:

\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{d-k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\mp,fs}\rightarrow\pi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{E}}.

and these pairings are compatible with Serre duality under the classicality theorem.

Proof.

We only prove the first point for
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}
. The other cases are similar. There is a succession of three spectral sequences, having as input the sheaves
\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{+,fs}
(for varying
k
and
w
) and finally converging to the classical cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}
.
The first spectral sequence is the Tor spectral sequence of theorem
6.5.2
, from the cohomology sheaf over
\mathcal{W}
to the locally analytic overconvergent cohomology in a given weight.
The second spectral sequence is the BGG spectral sequence of theorem
6.5.5
, from locally analytic overconvergent cohomology to overconvergent cohomology. The third spectral sequence is the spectral sequence of theorem
5.5.1
, from overconvergent to classical cohomology.
Therefore, starting from a classical class, we can lift it successively to the
E_{1}
terms of the last two spectral sequences (for suitable choices of
w_{M}
and
w^{M}
) and then to a class in a suitable
\mathrm{Tor}^{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}_{-p}(\mathrm{H}^{q}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{+,fs},k(\nu))
for
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
. Therefore,
(\lambda^{sm}_{p}\nu_{alg},\lambda^{S})
is in the support of
\mathrm{H}^{q}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{+,fs}
.

We now prove point
(2)
. Let
(\lambda_{p},\lambda^{S})
be a point in the support of
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}H^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{+,fs}
over a weight
\nu
. Then one sees that there is a corresponding class in
\oplus_{k}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}
. Indeed, take
k
maximal such that
(\lambda_{p},\lambda^{S})
is in the support of
\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}H^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{+,fs}
. The Tor spectral sequence implies that
(\lambda_{p},\lambda^{S})
is in the support of
\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}
.
Then we conclude by the control theorems (corollary
6.8.4
and theorem
5.12.3
):

\oplus_{k}\mathrm{H}^{k}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,sss_{w}(\nu)}=\oplus_{k}\mathrm{H}^{k}(K^{p},-w_{0,M}w(\nu_{alg}+\rho)-\rho,\chi)^{+,sss_{w}(\nu)}.

The last point follows from the functoriality of the pairing.
∎

We now define certain components of the eigenvariety of maximal dimension and show that they contain all finite slope interior cohomology classes. We first define sheaves:

\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{\pm,fs}=\mathrm{Im}({\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)^{\pm,fs}\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{\pm,fs}).

Proposition 6.9.4
.

(1)

The sheaves
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{\pm,fs}
are torsion free sheaves of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-modules.

(2)

For any
\nu:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
, the map
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{\pm,fs}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}k(\nu)\rightarrow\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{\pm,fs}
is surjective.

(3)

For any
\kappa_{alg}\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{M_{\mu},+}
and
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\overline{F}^{\times}
a finite order character, let
\nu=\nu^{alg}\chi
with
\nu^{alg}=-w^{-1}w_{0,M}(\kappa_{alg}+\rho)-\rho
. Then the map

\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{+}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,fs}\rightarrow\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{+}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}

is injective and the map

\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{-}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{-,fs}\rightarrow\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{-}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},-w_{0,M}\kappa-2\rho_{nc},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}

is surjective.

(4)

There is a pairing

\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{+}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{+,fs}\times\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{-}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})^{-,fs}\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}

compatible with the preceding maps and the pairings

\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{+}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}\times\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{-}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{-,fs}\rightarrow k(\nu)

and

\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{+}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,fs}\times\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{-}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},-w_{0,M}\kappa-2\rho_{nc},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}\rightarrow F.

Proof.

The first point follows from theorem
6.7.3
. The vanishing theorem and Tor spectral sequence imply that
{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un},cusp)^{\pm,fs}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}k(\nu)\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu,cusp)^{\pm,fs}
is an isomorphism, which gives the second point. The vanishing theorems and the BGG spectral sequence imply that
{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{+}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,fs}\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{+}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{+,fs}
is injective and

{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{-}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu,cusp)^{-,fs}\rightarrow\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{-}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},-w_{0,M}\kappa-2\rho_{nc},\chi^{-1},cusp)^{-,fs}
is surjective, which gives the third point follows. The last point is clear.
∎

Remark 6.9.5
.

There is an asymmetry between the
-
and
+
theories in point (3). This asymmetry results from our choice to develop the
+
theory using locally analytic induction sheaves and the
-
theory using their dual distribution sheaves.

We let
\mathcal{Z}^{!}_{w}
,
\mathcal{E}^{!}_{w}
be the supports of
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}
and
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu^{un})_{\mathcal{Z}}^{\pm,fs}
. We let
\mathcal{Z}^{!}
and
\mathcal{E}^{!}
be the union of the
\mathcal{Z}^{!}_{w}
and
\mathcal{E}^{!}_{w}
.

Theorem 6.9.6
.

The following holds:

(1)

The eigenvariety
\mathcal{E}^{!}
and the spectral variety
\mathcal{Z}^{!}
are either empty or equidimensional of dimension
\dim\mathcal{W}
. The same is true of the
\mathcal{E}^{!}_{w}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{!}_{w}
for each
w\in{}^{M}W
.

(2)

For all
w\in{}^{M}W
,
\kappa_{alg}\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{M,+}
, and
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\overline{F}^{\times}
a finite order character, let
\nu_{alg}=-w^{-1}w_{0,M}(\kappa_{alg}+\rho)-\rho
.
Any eigenclass in
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{-}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa^{\vee}_{alg},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}
or
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{+}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}
gives a point of
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{!}
of weight
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
.

(3)

Let
\kappa_{alg}\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{M,+}
and
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\overline{F}^{\times}
be a finite order character. For any eigenclass
c
in
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}
or
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg}^{\vee},\chi^{-1})^{-,fs}
there is a
w\in{}^{M}W
with
\ell_{\pm}(w)=i
so that
c
gives a point of
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{!}
of weight
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
where
\nu_{alg}=-w^{-1}w_{0,M}(\kappa_{alg}+\rho)-\rho
.

Proof.

This first two points follow from proposition
6.9.4
. The third point follows from the second point and corollary
5.8.1
.
∎

This theorem in particular implies that any finite slope system of Hecke eigenvalues in classical or overconvergent interior cohomology admits an analytic deformation of maximum dimension.

In light of point (3) of the theorem, it is natural to ask: given an eigenclass
c
occurring in
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,fs}
, what is the set
{}^{M}W(c)
of
w\in{}^{M}W
with
\ell_{+}(w)=i
for which
c
occurs in
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{i}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)
? It is natural to compare
{}^{M}W(c)
to the set
C(\kappa_{alg})^{+}
, i.e. the set of
w\in{}^{M}W
for which
\nu_{alg}+\rho\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{+}
.

Proposition 6.9.7
.

Assume that the eigenclass
c
satisfies
+,ss^{M}(\kappa_{alg})
. Then
{}^{M}W(c)\subseteq C(\kappa)^{+}
.

Proof.

By theorem
6.10.1
(2) below,
\overline{\mathrm{H}}_{w}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa_{alg},\chi)^{+,ss^{M}(\kappa)}=0
unless
w\in C(\kappa)^{+}
.
∎

In particular if
C(\kappa)^{+}
has only one element of length
i
(for example if
\kappa+\rho
is regular) then
{}^{M}W(c)
is determined under this small slope hypothesis.

Example 6.9.8
.

We give a basic counter-example to the proposition if we drop the small slope hypothesis. We assume the Shimura variety is compact, so that the constant function
1\in H^{0}(S_{K},\mathscr{O})
defines a finite slope interior cohomology class. Then
{}^{M}W(1)=\{Id\}
, and the corresponding point of the eigenvariety
\mathcal{E}
is in weight
\nu_{alg}=-w_{0,M}\rho-\rho=-2\rho_{nc}
, and so in particular
\nu_{alg}+\rho
is not dominant. On the other hand we have
C(0)^{+}=\{w_{0}^{M}\}
.

Remark 6.9.9
.

If
c
is a cohomology class represented by an automorphic representation
\pi
which is tempered at
\infty
and contributes to coherent cohomology in weight
\kappa
, then
\pi_{\infty}
is a limit of discrete series which is described by the pair consisting of its infinitesimal character
-\kappa-\rho
and a chamber
wX^{\star}(T)^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}\subseteq X^{\star}(T)^{M,+}_{\mathbb{Q}}
for
w\in C(\kappa)^{+}
(see
[
Har90a
]
, sect. 3.3). We can ask if this class lifts to a point in
\mathcal{E}_{w}^{!}
.

6.10.
Improved slope bounds for interior cohomology and applications

Using the interior eigenvariety we are able to prove that the conjectured slopes bounds
6.8.1
,
5.9.2
, and
5.10.7
hold for interior cohomology. The idea is that classical points in regular weight satisfy the correct slope bound by corollary
5.10.12
, and these points are dense in the interior eigenvariety.

Theorem 6.10.1
.

(1)

Fix
w\in{}^{M}W
and a locally algebraic weight
\nu=\nu_{alg}\chi
for
\nu_{alg}\in X^{\star}(T^{c})
and
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\overline{F}^{\times}
a finite order character. For any character
\lambda
of
T^{\pm}
on
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w,an}(K^{p},\nu)^{\pm,fs}
we have
v(\lambda)\geq 0
in the
+
case and
v(\lambda)\leq 0
in the
-
case.

(2)

Fix
w\in{}^{M}W
,
\kappa\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{M,+}
, and a finite order character
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to F^{\times}
. For any character
\lambda
of
T^{\pm}
on
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{\ell_{\pm}(w)}_{w}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{\pm,fs}
we have
v(\lambda)\geq w^{-1}w_{0,M}(\kappa+\rho)+\rho
in the
+
case and
v(\lambda)\leq w^{-1}(\kappa+\rho)-\rho
in the
-
case.

(3)

Fix
\kappa\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{M,+}
and a finite order character
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\overline{F}^{\times}
. Let
\nu=-w^{-1}w_{0,M}(\kappa+\rho)-\rho
for any
w\in C(\kappa)^{+}
. Then for any character
\lambda
of
T\pm
on
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{\pm,fs}
we have
v(\lambda)\geq-\nu
in the
+
case and
v(\lambda)\leq-w_{0}\nu
in the
-
case.

Proof.

In the
-
case, the first point implies the second point by theorem
6.9.6
(3). In the
+
case, one repeats the constructions of this section, exchanging the roles of the sheaves of locally analytic functions and distributions. The second point implies the third point as in the proof of proposition
5.10.9
using corollary
5.8.1
.

We now prove the first point. The eigensystem
\lambda
gives a point
(\nu,\lambda_{p},\lambda^{S})
of
\mathcal{E}^{!}
(where
\lambda_{p}=\lambda
in the
+
case and
\lambda^{t}
in the
-
case.) We can find another point
(\nu^{\prime},\lambda_{p}^{\prime},\lambda^{{}^{\prime}S})
which satisfies
v(\lambda^{\prime}_{p})=v(\lambda_{p})
and
\nu^{\prime}=\nu^{\prime}_{alg}\chi
is locally algebraic, and
\nu^{\prime}_{alg}\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{+}
is sufficiently large so that
v(\lambda^{\prime}_{p})
satisfies
+,sss(\nu)
. Then this point is classical by theorem
6.9.3
(2), and so the slope bound is satisfied by corollary
5.10.12
.
∎

As a consequence we deduce a vanishing theorem for interior cohomology which improves on theorem
5.12.5
.

Theorem 6.10.2
.

Let
\kappa\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{M,+}
and let
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\overline{F}^{\times}
be a finite order character. We have that
\overline{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{ss^{M}(\kappa)}
is supported in the range
[\ell_{\min}(\kappa),\ell_{\max}(\kappa)]
.

Proof.

Theorem
6.10.1
(2) implies that the complex
\overline{\mathcal{C}ous}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{\pm,ss^{M}(\kappa)}
is concentrated in the range
[\ell_{\min}(\kappa),\ell_{\max}(\kappa)]
, which implies the theorem by corollary
5.8.1

∎

As another application we are able to improve our classicality theorem for overconvergent cuspforms.

Theorem 6.10.3
.

Let
\kappa\in X^{\star}(T^{c})^{M,+}
satisfy
C(\kappa)^{+}=\{1\}
, and let
\chi:T^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\overline{F}^{\times}
be a finite order character. Then
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{Id}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi,cusp)^{+,ss^{M}(\kappa)}\to\mathrm{H}^{0}_{Id}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,ss^{M}(\kappa)}
factors through
\mathrm{H}^{0}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,ss^{M}(\kappa)}
.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{0}_{Id}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi,cusp)^{+,fs}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}ous^{1}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi,cusp)^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{0}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,fs}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{0}_{Id}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+,fs}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}ous^{1}(K^{p},\kappa,\chi)^{+}}

where the bottom row is exact by proposition
5.7.4
. After passing to the
+,ss^{M}(\kappa)
part of this diagram, the right vertical arrow is 0 by theorem
6.10.2
.
∎

Remark 6.10.4
.

Theorem
6.10.3
says that a small slope overconvergent cuspform comes from a classical eigenform. Although we have not proved it, this eigenform should also be cuspidal (for instance this would follow from conjeture
5.9.2
). We note that it is possible for an overconvergent cuspform which does not have small slope to come from a non cuspidal eigenform. A basic example is the non-ordinary
p
-stabilization of an Eisenstein series for
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
.

6.11.
Application: construction of Galois representations and local-global compatibility
p

Let
L
be a totally real or CM number field. The class of regular, algebraic, essentially (conjugate) self dual, cuspidal automorphic representations for
\mathrm{GL}_{n}/L
have been studied intensively. In particular, one can attach to them compatible systems of Galois representations which satisfy many of the expected properties (see, e.g.,
[
CH13
]
,
[
BLGGT14
]
):

Theorem 6.11.1

(Bellaiche, Caraiani, Chenevier, Clozel, Harris, Kottwitz,
Labesse, Shin, Taylor, …)
.

Let
\pi
be a regular, algebraic, essentially (conjugate) self dual, cuspidal automorphic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}/L
with
\pi^{c}=\pi^{\vee}\otimes\chi
and infinitesimal character
\lambda=((\lambda_{1,\tau},\ldots,\lambda_{n,\tau})_{\tau\in\mathrm{Hom}(L,\mathbb{C})})
with
\lambda_{1,\tau}>\cdots>\lambda_{n,\tau}
.
Then for each isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\simeq\mathbb{C}
there is a continuous
Galois representation
\rho_{\pi,\iota}:G_{L}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

such that:

(1)

\rho_{\pi,\iota}^{c}\simeq\rho_{\pi,\iota}^{\vee}\otimes\epsilon_{p}^{1-n}\otimes\chi_{\iota}
where
\chi_{\iota}
is the
p
-adic realization of
\chi
and
\epsilon_{p}
is the cyclotomic character.

(2)

\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is pure.

(3)

\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is de Rham at all places dividing
p
, with
\iota^{-1}\circ\tau
-Hodge-Tate weights:
(-\lambda_{n,\tau}+\frac{n-1}{2},\cdots,-\lambda_{1,\tau}+\frac{n-1}{2})
.

(4)

For all finite place

v
one has

\iota\mathrm{WD}(\rho_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{F_{v}}})^{F-ss}=\mathrm{rec}(\pi_{v}\otimes|\det|_{v}^{\frac{1-n}{2}}).

The Galois representations of this theorem are usually found in the étale cohomology of unitary Shimura varieties, while in the remaining cases they are constructed by
p
-adic interpolation.

In
[
FP19
]
, section 9, we defined a certain class of cuspidal automorphic representations for the group
\mathrm{GL}_{n}/L
which (when
L
is CM) realize in the coherent cohomology of unitary Shimura varieties, by weakening the condition of regular to weakly regular and odd. To these weakly regular, odd, algebraic, essentially (conjugate) self dual, cuspidal automorphic representations, one can still attach compatible system of Galois representations (
[
Box15
]
,
[
GK19
]
,
[
PS16
]
), but at present many of their expected properties are not known.
The techniques of this paper allows for a new construction of the Galois representation via analytic families. The advantage of this construction is that we can prove some instances of local-global compatibility at
p
, using results of Kisin
[
Kis03
]
on the interpolation of crystalline periods in analytic families, as in the work of Jorza and Mok
[
Jor12
]
,
[
Mok14
]
.

Theorem 6.11.2
.

Let
\pi
be a weakly regular, odd, algebraic, essentially (conjugate) self dual, cuspidal automorphic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}/L
with
\pi^{c}=\pi^{\vee}\otimes\chi
and infinitesimal character
\lambda=((\lambda_{1,\tau},\ldots,\lambda_{n,\tau})_{\tau\in\mathrm{Hom}(L,\mathbb{C})})
with
\lambda_{1,\tau}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{n,\tau}
.
Then for each isomorphism
\iota:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\simeq\mathbb{C}
there is a continuous
Galois representation
\rho_{\pi,\iota}:G_{L}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

such that:

(1)

\rho_{\pi,\iota}^{c}\simeq\rho^{\vee}\otimes\epsilon_{p}^{1-n}\otimes\chi_{\iota}
where
\chi_{\iota}
is the
p
-adic realization of
\chi
.

(2)

\rho_{\pi,\iota}
is unramified at all finite places
v\nmid p
for which
\pi_{v}
is unramified and one has

\iota WD(\rho_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{L_{v}}})^{F-ss}=\mathrm{rec}(\pi_{v}\otimes|\det|_{v}^{\frac{1-n}{2}}).

(3)

\rho_{\pi,\iota}
has generalized
\iota^{-1}\circ\tau
-Hodge–Tate weights
(-\lambda_{n,\tau}+\frac{n-1}{2},\cdots,-\lambda_{1,\tau}+\frac{n-1}{2})
.

(4)

Let
v\mid p
be a place of
L
and assume that
\pi_{v}
is a regular principal series (i.e. the Jacquet module
(\pi_{v})_{U}
is a direct sum of
n!
distinct characters of
T(L_{v})
). Then
\rho_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{L_{v}}}
is potentially crystalline and

\iota WD(\rho_{\pi,\iota}|_{G_{L_{v}}})^{F-ss}=\mathrm{rec}(\pi_{v}\otimes|\det|_{v}^{\frac{1-n}{2}}).

Proof.

The existence of a Galois representation satisfying points (1) and (2) is already known (see
[
FP19
]
, theorem 9.10). In order to prove points (3) and (4), by base change we may assume that
L
is CM with maximal totally real subfield
F
, all the primes above
p
in
F
split in
L
, and for all primes
v\mid p
of
L
,
\pi_{v}
is a constituent of a principal series representation (and so has finite slope in the sense of section
4.3
).

Let
\mathrm{GU}(n)/F
be the quasi-split unitary similitude group in
n
variables, and let
G\subset\mathrm{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}
be the subgroup where the similitude factor lands in
\mathbb{G}_{m}\subseteq\mathrm{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The group
G
admits a PEL Shimura datum
(G,X)
, so that
\pi
realizes in the interior coherent cohomology of the corresponding Shimura variety, as explained in the proof of theorem 9.11 of
[
FP19
]
(we note that this is conditional on the main results of
[
Mok15
]
).

The result now follows from theorems
6.9.6
and
6.11.1
, using the results of
[
Kis03
]
as explained in proof of theorem 4.1 of
[
Jor12
]
.
∎
